They mostly just criticize me... telling me I'm failing or that I'm responsible for bad things happening. But they haven't told me to actually take any action.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices constant when you're in the monitor room or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They definitely come and go. It's mostly when things get quiet in the room, like I mentioned before. If I'm busy or there's a lot going on, I don't hear them. But when there's a lull, that's when they start coming through the speakers, just... criticizing me, telling me I'm missing things and people will get hurt. Even though, like I said, the channels are muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not in the monitor room or when you're not near the speakers?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just... just in that room. Like I said, it's what scared me enough to finally come in and get help. It only happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room. I'll just be sitting there, and then I hear those voices coming right through the monitor speakers. They just keep saying, \"You missed it, Ruth,\" or... \"People are going to get hurt because of you.\" And it's so clear, but I know every single channel is muted. And the other guards, they don't hear a thing. It's only me, and it's only through those speakers at work.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices sound like they are coming from inside your head or from outside, like actually from the speakers?\n\nPatient: They sound like they are coming from the outside. Like I was saying, it sounds exactly like they are physically coming out of the monitor speakers. That is really what scared me enough to finally come in and see a doctor. \n\nIt doesn't sound like a thought inside my head at all. It sounds like a real audio feed coming right through the equipment, telling me you missed it, Ruth, or saying that people are going to get hurt because of me. It sounds completely real and outside of me, even though I know the channels are muted and none of the other guards hear a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there when you're alone in the monitor room or at any other time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't see anything. It's just the voices through the speakers. I haven't noticed anything visually that isn't supposed to be there, either at work or at home. It's really just the audio.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful or traumatic events in your life recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing stands out. Life has just been... normal, I guess. Just going to work, coming home. There hasn't been any big trauma or anything like that recently.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Life has just been... normal, I guess. Just going to work, coming home. There hasn't been any big trauma or anything like that recently.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices criticizing you?\n\nPatient: I just feel... horrible. Like they're right. I keep replaying every little thing in my head, you know? Every missed camera angle or anytime I was a second late making a call. It just feels like proof that I'm careless, like I'm dangerous to the people I'm supposed to be protecting. And honestly... part of me really believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch because I'm just so useless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days... it's just this heavy, gray feeling that settles over me before I even get out of bed in the morning. It's just hard to shake. Sometimes I'll be sitting in my car before my shift, just looking at the casino lights, and I'll start tearing up for no reason at all. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot, actually. I'd go to salsa class, play softball, build PCs... I even used to have people over for board games pretty often. But lately, I just... I don't care about any of it. People text me to come out or ask if I want to play, and I just ignore them. I just sit at home. It's like none of it can even reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about four months now. It's mostly when things get quiet in the room, like I mentioned before. If I'm busy or there's a lot going on, I don't hear them. But when there's a lull, that's when they start coming through the speakers, just... criticizing me, telling me I'm missing things and people will get hurt. Even though, like I said, the channels are muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not in the monitor room or when you're not near the speakers?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just... just in that room. I'll just be sitting there, and then I hear those voices coming right through the monitor speakers. It's just the voices through the speakers. I haven't noticed anything visually that isn't supposed to be there, either at work or at home. It just feels like proof that I'm careless, like I'm dangerous to the people I'm supposed to be protecting. And honestly... part of me really believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch because I'm just so useless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days... it's just this heavy, gray feeling that settles over me before I even get out of bed in the morning. It's just hard to shake. Sometimes I'll be sitting in my car before my shift, just looking at the casino lights, and I'll start tearing up for no reason at all. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot, actually. I just don't care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of pleasure in activities for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much all day, every day. It really feels like none of it can even reach me anymore, and it's been like that constantly for these four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy more recently or if it's been a gradual change over time?\n\nPatient: It wasn't really a gradual thing. Like I was just saying, it all seemed to drop off right around four months ago when I got that new role at work. Before then, I used to look forward to all of it—the salsa class, softball, building PCs, having people over for board games. But ever since that change, I just... I just ignore the texts and sit at home. It's stayed about the same over the last four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Even when it's quiet and I'm not hearing them, I just... I feel this heavy guilt about work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call in my head. It feels like proof that I'm careless, and honestly, dangerous. Part of me really believes that I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling sad or depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It sounds like a real audio feed coming right through the equipment, telling me you missed it, Ruth, or saying that people are going to get hurt because of me. It sounds completely real and outside of me, even though I know the channels are muted and none of the other guards hear a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there when you're alone in the monitor room or at any other time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't see anything. It's just the voices through the speakers. I haven't noticed anything visually that isn't supposed to be there, either at work or at home. It's really just the audio.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful or traumatic events in your life recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing stands out. Life has just been... normal, I guess. Just going to work, coming home. There hasn't been any big trauma or anything like that recently.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices criticizing you?\n\nPatient: I just feel... horrible. Like they're right. I keep replaying every little thing in my head, you know? Every missed camera angle or anytime I was a second late making a call. It just feels like proof that I'm careless, like I'm dangerous to the people I'm supposed to be protecting. And honestly... part of me really believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch because I'm just so useless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days... it's just this heavy, gray feeling that settles over me before I even get out of bed in the morning. It's just hard to shake. Sometimes I'll be sitting in my car before my shift, just looking at the casino lights, and I'll start tearing up for no reason at all. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot, actually. I'd go to salsa class, play softball, build PCs... I even used to have people over for board games pretty often. But lately, I just... I don't care about any of it. People text me to come out or ask if I want to play, and I just ignore them. I just sit at home. It's like none of it can even reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about four months now. It started right around the time I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't have any interest in those things anymore. The softball, the board games, all of that... it's all just stopped over these last four months. I just don't care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of pleasure in activities for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much all day, every day. Like I said, I used to really look forward to my salsa classes, playing softball, building PCs, and having friends over for board games. But now... I just ignore their texts. I just sit at home. It really feels like none of it can even reach me anymore, and it's been like that constantly for these four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy more recently or if it's been a gradual change over time?\n\nPatient: It wasn't really a gradual thing. Like I was just saying, it all seemed to drop off right around four months ago when I got that new role at work. Before then, I used to look forward to all of it—the salsa class, softball, building PCs, having people over for board games. But ever since that change, I just... I just ignore the texts and sit at home. It really just feels like none of it can reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any big changes with my weight. It's stayed about the same over the last four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Even when it's quiet and I'm not hearing them, I just... I feel this heavy guilt about work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call in my head. It feels like proof that I'm careless, and honestly, dangerous. Part of me really believes that I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling sad or depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone else, but never anything like this. This whole heavy feeling... it really just started around four months ago. Before that, I was always pretty steady. I've never gone through a period where I felt this low or just completely checked out of my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, depressed, or disconnected from things for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not two years. Like I just mentioned, it really only started about four months ago. Before that, everything was fine and I felt completely normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot, actually. I'd go to salsa class, play softball, build PCs... I even used to have people over for board games pretty often. But lately, I just... I don't care about any of it. People text me to come out or ask if I want to play, and I just ignore them. I just sit at home. It's like none of it can even reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about four months now. It started right around the time I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't have any interest in those things anymore. The softball, the board games, all of that... it's all just stopped over these last four months. I just don't care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of pleasure in activities for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much all day, every day. Like I said, I used to really look forward to my salsa classes, playing softball, building PCs, and having friends over for board games. But now... I just ignore their texts. I just sit at home. It really feels like none of it can even reach me anymore, and it's been like that constantly for these four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy more recently or if it's been a gradual change over time?\n\nPatient: It wasn't really a gradual thing. Like I was just saying, it all seemed to drop off right around four months ago when I got that new role at work. Before then, I used to look forward to all of it—the salsa class, softball, building PCs, having people over for board games. But ever since that change, I just... I just ignore the texts and sit at home. It really just feels like none of it can reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any big changes with my weight. It's stayed about the same over the last four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Even when it's quiet and I'm not hearing them, I just... I feel this heavy guilt about work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call in my head. Most days... it's just this heavy, gray feeling that settles over me before I even get out of bed in the morning. It's just hard to shake. Sometimes I'll be sitting in my car before my shift, just looking at the casino lights, and I'll start tearing up for no reason at all. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot, actually. I'd go to salsa class, play softball, build PCs... I even used to have people over for board games pretty often. But lately, I just... I don't care about any of it. It's like none of it can even reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about four months now. It started right around the time I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't have any interest in those things anymore. The softball, the board games, all of that... it's all just stopped over these last four months. I just don't care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of pleasure in activities for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much all day, every day. Like I said, I used to really look forward to my salsa classes, playing softball, building PCs, and having friends over for board games. But now... I just ignore their texts. I just sit at home. It really feels like none of it can even reach me anymore, and it's been like that constantly for these four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy more recently or if it's been a gradual change over time?\n\nPatient: It wasn't really a gradual thing. Like I was just saying, it all seemed to drop off right around four months ago when I got that new role at work. Before then, I used to look forward to all of it—the salsa class, softball, building PCs, having people over for board games. But ever since that change, I just... I just ignore the texts and sit at home. It really just feels like none of it can reach me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any big changes with my weight. It's stayed about the same over the last four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Even when it's quiet and I'm not hearing them, I just... I feel this heavy guilt about work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call in my head. It feels like proof that I'm careless, and honestly, dangerous. Part of me really believes that I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling sad or depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never gone through a period where I felt this low or just completely checked out of my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, depressed, or disconnected from things for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not two years.
